The transferred read owns the old pointer, so no old owner is destroyed twice, in every build mode.
- [NEW-CORE-F1-OVERFLOW-1] In a counted loop (`counter = phi(start, counter + 1)` while `counter < bound`), a header phi `acc = phi(init, next)` whose `next` is `acc` moved by checked or proven additions and subtractions, joins of such values inside one iteration (a conditional `count += 1`), and inner counted loops that move it (an inner loop's own such phi, entered from a value moved from `acc`) grows by a step in `[lo, hi]` per iteration, and so lies in `[init.lo + min(0, trips × lo), init.hi + max(0, trips × hi)]`, with `trips` the counted loop's trip bound (a list or byte length bounds it by `max_list_length`); an inner loop contributes its entry's step plus its own trips times its own step. A wrapping operation, a value not derived from `acc`, a loop that is not counted, or a walk of more than 64 values gives no interval. Checks the intervals rule out become `proven`; verify builds assert each interval (`R_CORE_FACT`). A fact pass that does not converge also counts every block reachable.
- [NEW-CORE-F1-OVERFLOW-2] A branch edge on `x * x <= y`, `x * x < y` or `x * x == y` (either side, a checked or proven product) bounds `x` to `[-r, r]` where it dominates, `r` the integer square root of `y`'s top (less one for `<`): `while i * i <= n` keeps `i` and `i + 1` and `j + i` (with `j <= n` from its own loop condition) in range, so their checks become `proven`.
- [NEW-CORE-F1-OVERFLOW-3] A release build's first fact pass (parameters unknown) runs callees before callers (a cycle is broken where it closes) and gives each internal function with an integer or boolean result the hull of the intervals of its reachable `ret` operands; every direct call of it then lies in that interval, in the first pass (so its argument intervals reach `NEW-CORE-FACTS-10`'s parameter intervals) and in the real one. `step(a, b) = (a + b) % 1000003` called as `total = step(total, i)` with `i >= 0` gets `a` in `[0, 1000002]`, its sum proven and its floor remainder unsigned with no fix-up; one call that may pass a negative argument keeps the fix-up. Verify builds assert each call's result interval.
- [NEW-CORE-F1-OVERFLOW-4] A `proven` (or unsigned) 64-bit division or remainder whose operands the trusted facts put in `[0, 2^32 − 1]` masks each operand to its low 32 bits (`and i64 x, 4294967295`, the same value) before its `udiv`/`urem`, so LLVM knows the high bits are zero and divides by a constant with the short reciprocal sequence; verify builds never narrow.
- [NEW-CORE-F1-OVERFLOW-5] `--sabotage-facts=overflow` builds verify mode with the overflow rules each one off on the inside — a counter's per-iteration step one smaller, `x * x <= y` bounding `x` by its square root less one, a callee's return interval without its top — and the verify assertions catch each of them (`R_CORE_FACT`) on a program that reaches the bound.
- [NEW-CORE-F2-WRAP-1] Checked list access tests the written index unsigned against the length, trapping negative or oversized indexes with the original value. The in-bounds address uses the written index directly and contains no from-end normalization branch or merge. Bounds proven by existing facts may still be omitted.
- [NEW-CORE-F2-INDEX-1] A counted fill loop's list length fact (`length = max(n, 0)`) holds only where the loop's exit dominates the access: inside the loop the list has as many elements as iterations so far, so a read of `zs[i]` before `zs.append(i)` keeps its check (main 35df839be's release builds proved it and read past the end). Where it holds, an index whose interval lies below the least value of `n` is in range (`flags[j]` with `j <= n` and `n + 1` flags).
- [NEW-CORE-F2-INDEX-2] Value and length keys (`index_keys.zig`): a field read of a record this function built or updated is the value stored there (through `record_set`s of other fields to the `record_set` or `record_make` that stored it); a phi whose operands on executable edges are one value (or the phi itself) is that value; an index key adds a constant moved by checked or proven additions and subtractions; a list's length key sees through `list_set`, `list_set_field`, `list_copy`, `list_sort` and phis whose operands all have one key. Keys never look through a phi of different values, so they relate values of one iteration only. A dominating branch edge `x < len(other)` (or `len(other) > x`, through value-preserving conversions) proves an access to a list with `other`'s length key at an index with `x`'s key that the facts put at or above zero: a list written through a copy, the iterated list of `for x in xs`, two reads of one record's list field.
- [NEW-CORE-F2-INDEX-3] After ownership, a list access whose check a dominating access already made becomes `proven` (`index_checks.zig`): the same length key and index key, and, when the access's index may be negative as a plain index, a dominating check that shows its sign (a plain index's check does; a written index's normalized check does not, unless the facts put the index at or above zero). It covers the read and field write ownership splits a whole-element write into, the swap after Hoare partition's scans, and a pop's push after reads of the same slot. A pop, a push, a field write of the index or a different list between the two accesses changes a key, and the later access keeps its check. Verify builds assert each such check (`R_CORE_FACT`).
- [NEW-CORE-F2-INDEX-4] `--sabotage-facts=index` builds verify mode with the dominating-check rule taking a check to cover the next index up too; the verify assertion catches it (`R_CORE_FACT`) on `xs[i]` then `xs[i + 1]` with `i` the last index.
- [NEW-CORE-F2-INDEX-5] An access whose list's length key and index key both name one phi `p` (a field of it, or `p` itself), whose block dominates the access, is also covered when, on the edge from each predecessor of `p`'s block, a check made in that predecessor or a block dominating it has the keys `p`'s operand on that edge gives (`rt.frames[rt.frame_count - 1]` at the top of the Lox VM's dispatch loop: read before the loop, and at the end of each pass). Entering the block, `p` is that operand, and nothing defines it again before the access; each covering check runs before the access it covers, so the first failure would have to be an earlier check.
- [NEW-CORE-F2-FIELD-1] A release build gives each integer or boolean field of a closed record type a whole-program interval (`fields.zig`): the hull of every value the program's writes store into it — `record_make` (each field), `record_set` (a `mut` callee's included) and `list_set_field` (a field inside a list element) — each at its definition under the first fact pass's facts. Records are values, so moving one into or out of a list, dictionary, box, union, array, phi, call or return copies it whole and writes no field. Two rounds run, the second under the first's intervals; each round's hulls hold whatever the previous round knew, and a field no write reaches has none. A record value the function has no view of its own (a parameter, a call's result, an element read) starts from its type's intervals, which its field updates and joins then carry (`Aggregates.fields`); a field read (`record_get`, or a `list_get_field` whose path ends in a record field) takes the interval (met with the function's own view of the record): `table[s.index]` with every `Slot.index` written as `k % 4` (`k >= 0`) needs no normalization from the end and, on a four-element table, no check. Verify builds assert every read's interval (`R_CORE_FACT`).
- [NEW-CORE-F2-FIELD-2] A record type is closed only when every record of it is made by the program: no runtime, extern or host function takes or returns a value containing it, the entry function's parameters and the parameters of every function a function value names (it may be called from outside) do not contain it, and no operation other than the copying ones (`phi`, `copy`, calls, `record_*`, list, union, box, array and dictionary element operations, `param`, `undef`, `func_ref`) produces a value containing it. A record inside an open record is open too. An open type's fields have no interval.
- [NEW-CORE-F2-FIELD-3] `--sabotage-facts=field` builds verify mode with each field interval's top dropped; the verify assertion catches it (`R_CORE_FACT`) where a read sees the largest value written.
- [NEW-CORE-F2-VERSION-1] Bounds versioning (`version.zig`), after the trip-count guards: a counted loop (`i` from `S` while `i < N`, step one, `S` and `N` fixed for the loop, and `N` not computed inside an enclosing loop: a bound that is data, such as fannkuch's flip count, is often a few trips that the guard's cost outweighs) whose body makes no call, holds no other loop and has at most 256 instructions (NEW-CORE-FSX-VERSION-1's rule), whose header carries only integers, floats and booleans and which leaves only those to the code after it (a copy that carries a list or record out gives ownership joins to reason through: a function of such copies took minutes to compile), and checks list accesses at `I + i`, `i - I` or `I - i` (checked or proven arithmetic, after a folded normalization from the end too) with `I` fixed for the loop (defined outside it, or an unchecked or proven sum, difference or product of such values, recomputed before the loop) and the list derived, through element writes, copies and sorts that keep its length, from the list the loop is entered with, is cloned behind one guard before it. The guard measures that entry list (the entry value must remain live through the guard) and tests, for every such access, that its least index over the trips `S..N-1` is at least 0 and its greatest below the length; a value the facts do not put within `[-2^60, 2^60]` is tested to lie there too, so the guard's unchecked sums are exact whenever they decide. The clone runs with those checks proven and their normalizations from the end folded away; the original, unchanged, runs when the guard fails, so a loop that would trap traps at the same trip with the same report, after the same output. The facts run again after.
- [NEW-CORE-F2-VERSION-2] `--sabotage-facts=version` builds verify mode with each bounds guard admitting one index past the end; the verify assertion of the clone's proven check catches it (`R_CORE_FACT`) on a loop that reaches that index.
- [NEW-CORE-FSX-VERSION-1] A release build versions a counted reduction loop under a trip-count guard (`version.versionReductions`) only when the loop's body makes no `call` or `call_indirect` and contains no other loop. The clone saves one overflow check per trip, which pays only where LLVM can then vectorize or unroll a small body; around a call or an inner loop it saves nothing measurable, and the duplicated body changes LLVM's code choices (a sum of binary-search results lost its conditional moves and ran 3.7 times slower). Such a loop keeps its exact check.
- [NEW-CORE-U1-GROW-1] A large byte-buffer block grows with realloc, as a large list does. realloc may preserve or move storage and may copy its contents; a result that loses 32-byte alignment is copied to a fresh aligned block. A byte buffer retains its doubling policy (at least eight octets, doubled until the request fits), existing length and octets; a small buffer grows into a fresh block. Neither copy-free remapping nor unchanged page residency is guaranteed. A counting build records each successful large-buffer growth under `reallocs`, and under `realloc_moves` when the final block address differs from the original; small-buffer allocate-and-copy growth remains under `buffer_grows`.
The reference-count uniqueness-hoisting rule NEW-CORE-U1-HOIST-1 was retired on 2026-10-17 with reference counting; checked ownership admits mutations of the sole owner.
- [NEW-CORE-U1-STATE-1] A release build reads a scalar field (an integer, float, `bool` or capability) of a record from the value last put there instead of the record's memory (`forward.zig`, after ownership): through `record_set`s of other fields, from the value a `record_set` of that field or a `record_make` stored, and through a phi of records as a new phi of that field's values. A record that is none of those (a parameter, a call's result, an element read) has its field read once, right where it is defined. So a loop that reads and writes `frame.ip` or `rt.stack_top` carries it in a register; the record's memory still receives every store, so calls that are handed the record, copies of it, returns and traps see every field as the program left it. Each new value gets the interval the facts know for its record's field.
- [NEW-CORE-U1-STATE-2] The emitter keeps the length and capacity of a list or byte buffer that a loop appends to in two entry-block slots, which LLVM promotes to registers (`emit_state.zig`): a *web* is the phis and in-place updates (`list_push` other than counted fills and stack pushes, `buffer_append`) that make one value from the one before, kept when it has a phi in a loop, every update updates a member, and inside the loops its phis run in nothing but those updates and phis uses a member. A value entering the web on an edge that jumps to the phi has its header loaded into the slots there; an update appends at the slots' length (a push that must grow stores the length back, calls `myl_core_list_grow` and loads the grown capacity; a buffer append's slow path stores the length back before `myl_core_buffer_append_checked` and loads the result's header after); every other use of a member (a read, a call, a release, a return, a phi outside the web) and every verify-build fact check of one is preceded by a store of the slots' length into its header.
- [NEW-CORE-U1-SINK-1] An element store that `NEW-CORE-CE2-EFFECTS-3` sinks into the successors that observe it is weighed again where each copy lands, up to 48 blocks down, so a store ahead of an if-elif ladder (a VM's `rt.frames[rt.frame_count - 1] = frame` after each opcode read) ends in the arms that read the list, return it, or pass it to a call that may read it, and the other arms go without. A store whose two successors both observe it still moves into both when one of them branches, within three levels, to an arm that does not. Every bounds check stays where the store was; the copies, which that check covers for the same list and index, check nothing again. Sinking preserves native output, traps and the lifetime of every stored operand without reference-count uniqueness tests.

- [JUICE-NATIVE-1] `selka juice` implements doctor, analyze, record, compare, validate and calibrate inside the installed compiler without Python or a source checkout. Capture validates declared workload contracts, immutable raw evidence, complete instruction counts, correctness, separate semantic counters and bounded process groups; reports distinguish instruction work, uncertainty and held-out runtime estimates. Platform-specific capture services remain separate from JSON reporting.
- [JUICE-CONTENT-HASH-1] Native Juice computes standard SHA-256 over every actual source, tool and running executable byte using a bounded streaming implementation compiled independently in ReleaseFast, including inside a Debug compiler. Digests remain independent of file size, timestamps, embedded source identity and previous invocations; same-size writes with restored mtime and atomic replacements are reread.

- [JUICE-RUNTIME-1] Juice’s opt-in perf runtime profile collects grouped, unscaled user instructions, cycles and reference cycles on one pinned CPU. Every event must be positive, present exactly once and report 100% running; otherwise capture remains incomplete. Comparisons keep instruction work separate from the reference-cycle runtime proxy, abstain above 3% observed reference-cycle spread (or paired-ratio spread for at least five explicitly matched, complete capture pairs) and never label a prediction as validated. `validate-runtime` checks the fixed 5% relative-error target using at least five independent paired elapsed timings tied to the same comparison, workload and context; uncertain timings or estimates cannot pass. Validation applies only to the measured case.

## Readable frontend

- [FRONTEND-NUMBERS-1] `parse_int(text)` and `parse_float(text)` parse a string using the pinned numeric grammar and return `int?` and `float?`. `int(number)` and `float(number)` convert numbers. Applying a numeric conversion to text reports `E_NUMERIC_PARSE` with an exact callee-name repair; parsing a numeric argument is a type error. Declared functions and bindings can shadow these prelude names.

- [FRONTEND-MIGRATION-1] Frontend migrations publish structured diagnostic edits from parsed spans. Growable arrays use `T[]`, optional layers use `T?`, fixed arrays use `FixedArray[T, N]`, and the unit type is `Unit`. Parameters put `mut` and individual `named` markers before their names. Comments, strings, unrelated names, canonical source whitespace, and optional nesting survive migration.
- [FRONTEND-FORMAT-1] Formatting keeps contextual expression words (`try`, `then`, `yield`, `fail`) separated from their operands and preserves the spacing between optional result types and failure clauses; formatting remains parse-only.
- [FRONTEND-FUNC-1] Function declarations and callable types use `func`; structured migration edits replace legacy `def` tokens without rewriting strings or comments. Project entry discovery and guest test discovery recognize `func` declarations, including public declarations.
- [FRONTEND-BINDINGS-1] Local declarations use `const` or `mut`; assignment updates an existing writable binding. An undeclared assignment is diagnosed with a declaration repair.
- [FRONTEND-BINDINGS-2] Locals have lexical block scope and may not shadow active locals or parameters. Independent sibling blocks may reuse names.
- [FRONTEND-MUT-1] Const ordinary data and ordinary parameters are transitively readonly, including nested fields, collection receivers and mut arguments. Loop and pattern binders are readonly. Capability permissions remain independent of binding mutability.
- [FRONTEND-MUT-2] Explicit mut parameters and arguments update caller storage for ordinary scalars, records and collections, including whole-value rebinding and nested field or element places. Readonly copies preserve value independence.
- [FRONTEND-MUT-3] Statically overlapping mut arguments are rejected. Dynamic indices and keys are captured once and checked for overlap before the callee runs, with disjoint targets allowed.
- [FRONTEND-TYPES-1] Postfix `T[]` is a growable array and `T?` an optional layer; suffix order and nested optional layers are preserved. `Unit` has value `()`. Fixed arrays use the explicit `FixedArray[T, N]` type and constructor. Present values inject into an expected optional without erasing existing layers.
- [FRONTEND-FAILURES-1] A function declared `-> T fails E` returns a checked Result value: ordinary returns construct success and `fail` constructs failure. Statement-boundary `try` unwraps success or propagates a compatible declared failure.
- [FRONTEND-MATCH-1] Enum construction and match statements name payload fields, and nested patterns are checked exhaustively. Value-producing match/yield expressions report E_MATCH_EXPRESSION_REMOVED with certain statement repairs. Continuing cases assign a common typed result, while return still leaves the enclosing function.
- [FRONTEND-NAMED-CALLBACK-1] Callable contracts retain named parameter labels and exact mutation positions, with positional binder names excluded from identity. Calls through a callable bind labelled operands to their declared parameters while evaluating arguments once in written order.

- [FRONTEND-RECORD-FIELDS-1] Record constructors name every supplied field. Known positional record or enum operands and patterns receive atomic label-insertion fixes that preserve written evaluation order, including imported types. Defaults retain their declared field identity.
- [FRONTEND-ARENA-REMOVED-1] Removed arena syntax reports a hard error with its certain source edits, including alongside other semantic errors; stores, returns, nested scopes and escapes migrate to accepted ordinary statements without changing their values.
- [FRONTEND-BINDING-MIGRATION-2] Implicit-binding declaration migration resolves same-named reads by lexical binding identity and initialization order; independent sibling blocks repair and converge, while reads outside all defining scopes or before initialization have no speculative repair.
- [FRONTEND-VARIANT-MIGRATION-1] Legacy variant payload migration preserves case newlines and comments and renders nested payload types canonically without overlapping edits.
- [FRONTEND-NESTED-COMPREHENSION-MIGRATION-1] Nested legacy array comprehensions migrate from inner to outer across fix iterations, preserving expressions and converging without overlapping edits.
- [FRONTEND-BYTES-PARENS-1] Byte literals preserve managed bytes identity through parentheses and inferred or contextual conditional arms, including unequal byte lengths; explicit fixed-array lengths and incompatible arm types still reject.
- [FRONTEND-NUMERIC-MIGRATION-2] A text-conversion repair may replace int or float with parse_int or parse_float only when the replacement resolves to the builtin parser. User functions, types, parameters, local bindings and import aliases with that spelling withhold that bare-name edit, including in generic bodies. An ordinary body then takes the method form instead (FRONTEND-NUMERIC-MIGRATION-3); a generic body, whose checker does not accept the str method, carries no fix and an actionable diagnostic.
- [FRONTEND-NUMERIC-MIGRATION-3] Where the bare parse_int or parse_float name is claimed in an ordinary function body, the E_NUMERIC_PARSE repair replaces the whole call with the str method form, `operand.parse_int()` or `operand.parse_float()`, and parenthesizes an operand that is not a name, field, call, method call, index, slice or string. No declaration can shadow that method, so a wrapper named for the builtin, such as the EDA port's `parse_int(s: str)`, no longer becomes a call of itself; the method builds the same Core IR as the bare call.
- [FRONTEND-DEVELOPER-PROJECT-1] Developer typed/IR dumps, coverage and Core checking resolve the same configured module roots, source budgets, embedded-file boundaries and import policies as normal builds; rejected inputs do not emit misleading successful dumps.

- [FRONTEND-CORE-NOALLOC-1] `@noalloc` declarations are build assertions over ownership-expanded Core before optimization. Check and check-project do not compute this Core contract. Build, run, build --run and test inspect unused concrete bounded bodies, cold blocks and transitive callees; a violated declaration reports E_NOALLOC_ALLOCATES at its allocation witness and fails before execution. Unsupported concrete bodies and unresolved foreign/callback paths are conservative errors. Lint and cost retain their static allocation analysis. Stack storage is permitted; compiler allocation and process startup are outside the assertion.
- [FRONTEND-ALLOCATION-SUMMARY-1] A summary request that falls back to concrete bodies after a new or changed written noalloc declaration still publishes fresh summaries on success. A subsequent unrelated edit can stand in unchanged bodies again, with effects, mutation facts and diagnostics identical to a cold check. Validation-only check requests likewise keep publishing after bounded-body edits.

- [NEW-CORE-U2-BORROW-1] A nested field borrow may be re-anchored in a consuming update’s result only when every field in its path is unchanged and that result dominates the use. Overlapping writes, whole-value replacement, unknown calls and phi edges are not forwarded. Source programs needing the earlier field across mutation use an explicit independent copy; trapping evaluation order is preserved.
- [NEW-CORE-U2-RELEASE-1] Generated destruction callbacks visit owned children at constant offsets with guards for active union payloads; dictionary callbacks distinguish whole entries, keys and values. An iterative pending chain destroys deep recursive values without exhausting the C stack. Specialized single-pointer element loops destroy each child directly. Destruction frees each owned child and its containing storage exactly once; no count, publication or shared-layout path remains.
- [NEW-CORE-U2-LOX-1] A signed constant addition or subtraction using a phi value or record field is proven safe only when the identical operation on the corresponding value was checked on every incoming edge, or that edge supplies a safe constant. A check from a loop iteration may cover its unchanged backedge by induction, with a separately covered entry; no positive interval is assumed for a mutable frame count. Verify-facts restores the arithmetic check.
- [NEW-CORE-U2-LOX-2] A bounded read-only list projection with one return and no loop may inline its complete control flow, preserving checked arithmetic, written-index bounds traps, list bounds checks, and inline trap ancestry.
- [NEW-CORE-U2-FRAME-1] A frame copied from a list remains an independent value across disjoint runtime updates, later writes to that list, and writeback of the frame. A live alias preserves the original fields; a borrowed list-element address may replace the copy only when its entire use interval excludes writes, moves, and releases of its storage.

- [NEW-CORE-S2-SPLIT-1] A private iteration over an explicit-separator text split may visit borrowed pieces lazily without an intermediate list, including leading, adjacent and trailing empty pieces. Unicode lengths and separator validation keep their original semantics. A piece that escapes is materialized, and ownership keeps every borrowed source alive and unchanged.
- [NEW-CORE-S2-SPLIT-2] A nonescaping split list may store offset/length pairs and derive borrowed pieces for read-only uses, preserving indexed bounds checks, Unicode scalar lengths and integer parsing grammar. A piece passed to storage or an unsupported consumer materializes before that use.
- [NEW-CORE-S2-SPLIT-3] A whitespace split loop inserting a materialized borrowed key may reuse a lookup probe only for the same unchanged dictionary and equivalent key. Materializing an owned key preserves provenance and the required re-probe when that equivalence is not established.
- [NEW-CORE-S2-VIEW-1] Read-only byte-string slices may borrow octet spans with the same nonnegative clamping and negative-bound diagnostic/trap rules as copied byte slices, without Unicode interpretation. Source-update guards and ownership preserve value semantics and lifetime; plain debug retains copies.
- [NEW-CORE-S2-SEARCH-1] Forward search and nonoverlapping count share the bounded span search: single octets use memchr and repeated multi-octet candidates fall back to the bounded two-way search. Scalar search windows, empty needles, prefixes, suffixes and replace sizing preserve their original semantics. Replace already sizes one output buffer before copying.
- [NEW-CORE-S2-SPLIT-4] A private split with only fixed nonnegative indexed reads may keep a bounded offset prefix in its function frame. The source-update guard applies, every short-input access retains its original bounds check, and a list length observable outside unreachable negative-index adjustment blocks disallows this representation.

- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-CACHE-1] Intermediate stage artifacts bind length-delimited inputs to the complete compiler identity and a stage schema. Atomic records include their key and payload checksum; missing, damaged, malformed, oversized, and unavailable entries lose reuse and compute fresh. Verification builds bypass intermediate caches.
- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-PACK-1] Intermediate stage records are published in complete checksummed journal frames, then sealed into immutable packs with verified indexes and independently bounded compressed blocks. Publication preserves read-your-writes and independent-store visibility; interrupted tails are misses, and only a complete pack replaces its journal. Concurrent writers serialize publication and newer records supersede earlier values for the same key. A successful save attempts sealing after its journal reaches 4 MiB, after releasing publication locks; failed cache I/O leaves the published journal available, while allocation failures remain explicit. Corruption, file loss after admission, invalid lengths, and unavailable storage retain conservative cache recovery. `SELKA_STAGE_CACHE_COMPRESSION=none` writes raw blocks for diagnostic comparisons; the default `zlib` policy, grouping, stage identities, and reads of either encoding are unchanged, and invalid values fail with a configuration diagnostic. Successful builds flush pending packs before publishing their build-replay receipt, and JSON cache reports expose this final maintenance time separately from stage times.
- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-CACHE-2] Function-stage artifacts guard numeric function/type catalogs, normalize literal references and mapped source line/column positions, and restore lists with exact owned capacities against the current source. Earlier same-line length edits preserve following functions' debugger and error-trace locations. Changed file lengths and literal-pool numbering do not invalidate unrelated functions; invalid locations and operand indexes miss safely.
- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-PACK-2] Packed-stage filename lookup uses a retained index with stable file IDs. Journal payload checksums and pack preparation, compression, and temporary-file writes occur outside the interprocess publication lock. Each bounded pack commit revalidates both its open journal and current path against the captured inode, length, modification/status times and compiler-bound header before publishing. The journal remains available until all chunks commit; damage or replacement during preparation rejects publication, and independent newer records retain sequence precedence. Allocation failures remain explicit and partial files are removed. Deterministic publication counters expose filename lookups, refreshes, unlocked preparation bytes and sequence-lock acquisitions without changing stage keys or block encodings.
- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-PACK-3] A process indexes every valid pack of its compiler, and each process seals its journal into a pack of its own, so a cache directory shared by many short-lived processes grows without bound unless packs are merged. A sealing process that finds at least 16 valid packs reads the 12 smallest (at most 32 MiB decoded), keeps only the records that are still the newest value of their key and verify against their checksums, writes them as one pack outside the publication lock, then under the lock confirms every source still has the status it was read with, publishes the merged pack, removes the sources and advances the sequence counter. A source that changed or vanished since it was read (another process merged it) aborts the merge and changes nothing; a pack that vanished is dropped from the catalog. Merged records keep their sequence numbers, so the newest value of a key wins across packs, journals and merges. Reuse is the only thing a failed or raced merge loses, and a record whose pack was removed is a miss, as any lost file is.
- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-CACHE-3] Lowering artifacts key a canonical dereferenced checked function tree, the ordered body-independent checked declaration/type catalog including generic instances and inferred summaries, resolved embedded contents, debug dropping and compiler identity. A rolling physical discovery guard preserves function/type IDs without hashing the growing module per body. Catalog and discovery guards normalize numeric lambda source offsets while retaining ordered IDs and signatures; restored bound declarations take fresh checked names and spans, while actual lambda body keys retain their complete names. Replay restores only each function's declaration/type/string discovery delta in order, including unused strings and compiler rendering authority names, relocates local spans and mapped external spans, and runs the existing module transforms afterwards. Damaged or invalid artifacts fall back to fresh lowering with the same refusals; the uncached builder remains an oracle for full IR and LLVM text.
- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-CACHE-4] Persistent optimizer function stages execute in the original mutation order and bind bodies, source ancestry, consulted callee bodies, available callee return intervals, caller-derived parameter hulls, effects, mode and pass events. Unchanged stages restore portable IR and retained final facts, ownership, vector plans and exact statistics; ordinary body edits reuse independent functions while direct, recursive and caller-parameter dependencies invalidate their consumers. Exploratory field analysis retains two rounds of sparse per-function reachable writes and type-opening/function-reference evidence. Reuse requires exact input field-table and consulted return identities. Final-stage keys bind the canonical whole-program field table; changed field dependencies restore validated pre-fact checkpoints before recomputation. Uncached passes remain an exact output oracle; malformed artifacts miss and decoded intermediate storage is reclaimed per function. Ordinary optimized development builds without whole-LLVM or statistics output retain validated durable fact handles separately from final IR metadata and decode dense facts only on LLVM unit misses; freshly computed facts remain available in memory even if publication fails. Eager and deferred analysis identities match; cached facts are verified before applying cached IR, and artifacts lost after admission trigger a conservative retry.
- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-CACHE-6] Native compilation respects both the requested worker count and a byte admission budget estimated from input size, with one oversized unit permitted alone. Workers use private reclaiming scratch and return diagnostics to the coordinator in input order; they never allocate on its arena concurrently. JSON reports expose stage reuse and inclusive stage latency plus actual linker-process time separately.
- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-CACHE-7] Successful ordinary builds release frontend syntax, interfaces and checked trees after lowering and before backend analysis. Physical IR tables remain on the result arena; borrowed function names, literal strings, source paths, line indexes, mapped origins, embedded contents and entry labels are copied there first. Cached and fresh LLVM, source positions and inline ancestry remain unchanged, and allocation failure releases the frontend exactly once.
- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-CACHE-8] A compact optimizer index preserves exploratory call/return summaries, incoming parameter hulls and final artifact keys for unchanged functions whose post-fusion input and dependency closure match. Ordered leaf-inline, CFG-inline, unswitch and parameter-inline checkpoints retain exact output identities, portable inline ancestry, pass counts and stage-specific call/reference graphs, including nonidentity transformations. A dirty caller observes the callee checkpoint that physical pass order would have produced; identity transformations alias their preceding checkpoint. These functions bypass earlier stage lookups and reconstruction; compact lowering discovery headers admit unchanged raw bodies without decoding instructions, and dirty callers request checkpoint bodies only when inspected. Final IR and analysis remain artifact handles until emission needs them. Demand keys guard declaration layout, semantic lowering identity, and source locations. Bodies with local replay positions bind the current original path, declaration line/column, and exact source prefix through the maximum replayed local offset; that maximum participates in the key. Unsupported nonlocal positions retain conservative whole-file guards, and invalid source bounds lose demand reuse. A missing deferred artifact retries once with eager frontend checking and artifact restoration, repairing the index; repeated failure is an invalid IR error. A reverse raw direct-call/function-reference graph invalidates callers of changed inputs. Changed incoming parameter hulls demote candidates and restore their pre-parameter checkpoint and accumulated pass counts before parameter-dependent optimization. Changed fusions retain portable post-fusion base checkpoints; unchanged fusions alias raw lowering. Compact ordered runtime declaration deltas are replayed only when dirty functions retain their exact ordered helper-creation requests, including requests for existing declarations, and the complete declaration schema and layout. Changed request sequences trigger eager recovery. Unchanged recursive functions may remain deferred when their exploratory summary validates the exact current ordered callee-return inputs. Record-bearing builds validate both field-summary rounds against those inputs and the field-table identity, then check the final field hulls. Changed exploratory inputs resume exact pre-fact checkpoints before recomputation; incoming parameter hulls must also match. Missing summary receipts, changed declaration layouts and missing or malformed artifacts retain the ordered stage-cache path; final IR, facts, ownership, pass statistics and relocated debugger ancestry match uncached optimization.
- [NEW-CORE-DEMAND-1] A development build may omit an unchanged ordinary checked body only when a verified lowering receipt admits its stable declaration, dependency context and source region. The lowering replay still validates its ordered catalogs and discoveries; a reached omitted body whose artifact is absent, damaged or invalid restarts complete frontend checking before fresh lowering. Receipts for nonlocal debug locations additionally prove the touched original-file identities and exact bytes, including locations synthesized during lowering. Bounded allocation-contract bodies and their transitive callees remain concrete for diagnostic checks. Cached and fresh IR, LLVM source positions and warnings agree across body edits, moved sources and effect changes.

- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-CACHE-9] Ordinary optimized-debug builds may pass unchanged LLVM units to the native driver as artifact handles. Compact receipts preserve exact names and runtime dependency flags. A settings-bound receipt locates the original text-derived native object key, including mode, CPU, tool and runtime ABI; the native object checksum is still verified. Only a missing object or explicit cache verification requires reloading LLVM and checking its exact key. Missing or damaged LLVM then triggers conservative recovery; a verified native object remains usable without LLVM. Freshly emitted LLVM remains usable when artifact publication fails.

- [NEW-CORE-R-ARRAY-1] Fixed arrays over four words are indexed in their existing storage. An element write reuses the old array home only when the memory-home planner proves that no live snapshot or operand aliases bytes it overwrites; otherwise the new snapshot is copied once. Index bounds, negative-index traps, and managed-element ownership remain unchanged.
- [NEW-CORE-R-SORT-1] The new-core native 64-bit integer engine uses insertion for at most 32 values, the adaptive stable merge engine for 33 through 256 values, and stable byte-radix partitions above 256, skipping constant bytes and already ordered inputs. Equal integer words retain their order; signed keys flip their sign bit for ordering. Packed keyed-sort words use the same engine and retain their original-position tie-breaker. Other types and reverse sorting keep the stable merge engine. Scratch allocation precedes every radix write and never allocates managed memory.
- [NEW-CORE-R-SRET-1] Sret and readonly memory arguments retain their separate noalias parameters, including functions passed as values. Destination/input sharing stays disabled after the sole-pointer prototype removed copies but increased record_pass instructions; its measurements and LLVM are retained as experiment evidence.
- [NEW-CORE-R-PUSH-1] Fallible record producers keep ordinary contiguous noalias sret storage. Whole-result reads, differently sized forwarded union variants, wider managed errors, independently copied or growing lists, and error cleanup after destination-list destruction preserve values and ownership. The payload-destination prototype is retained in experiment history: conservative lifetime checks left parse_list P6 unchanged at 444,833,472 bytes and added 30.70% total EDA record-copy bytes, so the ABI is not enabled.
- [NEW-CORE-R-ARRAY-2] Fixed-array literal elements seed an integer hull, element replacement widens that hull, and SSA joins use the existing convergent aggregate analysis. A read inherits only the proven hull. A normalized index whose interval lies within the fixed type's length needs no bounds trap; verify builds retain the trap and assert inherited scalar intervals.

- [NEW-CORE-JUICE-PLAN-1] Native Juice planning exports normalized selected workload contracts, exact expanded sources and inputs, compiler executable contents, build tools and declared dependencies, measurement configuration, CPU/OS context and expected workspace paths. It creates no workspace, takes no cache lock, builds nothing and executes no workload. Tool identity probes remain permitted. Input scopes include native glob bases and explicit dependency directories; prebuild library identity and undeclared provider closure remain explicitly incomplete.
- [NEW-CORE-COST-QUERY-1] Native static-cost queries reuse final Core function counts only under the current structural catalog, allocation provenance, pass events, reachability and verification mode. Existing compiler-stage queries and successful exact-unit LLVM remarks may be reused under their complete tool and emission identities. Supplied counters and remarks remain live inputs; missing or damaged query records compute fresh, failed remarks remain unknown, and cache verification uses the independent fresh oracle. Current-work reports distinguish computed, reused and verified queries without inferring machine cost or elapsed time.

- [NATIVE-WORK-CLAIM-1] Native compilation and linking acquire exact content-key work claims, recheck completed cache entries after waiting, and release claims on failure or process exit; waiters hold retention leases and independent keys retain parallel work.

- [NATIVE-TEST-COALESCE-1] Concurrent portable native test executions acquire unique exact identity claims in sorted order, recheck receipts and current sources after waiting, and retain claims through publication or failure. Claims retain cache storage without holding publication locks during execution; forced reruns invalidate receipts under the claim before executing so a failed rerun cannot supply an earlier success to followers.

- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-1] Checked ownership is the sole memory model. Owning lists, strings, bytes, buffers and aggregate fields move; ordinary calls borrow and mutable borrows are exclusive. Explicit independent copies duplicate owned dynamic children. Resources, scoped views, tasks and scalar foreign calls follow their checked contracts; unsupported shapes are errors, with no alternative memory model.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-2] Admitted zero-RC values are destroyed directly, including runtime arguments and nested children. Static text uses a storage-kind tag rather than a count. Text growth, owned slicing and explicit copies preserve values without any reference-count reads, writes or uniqueness tests, in debug and optimized builds.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-3] Compilation admits only audited runtime helper ownership contracts and verified owner lifetimes. The IR and emitted LLVM contain no reference-count operations. Whole-execution allocation counters and leak checks include startup and teardown; unsupported ownership lowering fails before execution.

- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-VIEWS-1] Under checked ownership, text and octet owners produce nonowning `TextView` or `ByteView` ranges with `.borrow(start, end)`. View bindings are immutable; reads and copies cannot follow owner mutation or movement. A function returning a view has exactly one immutable view parameter and returns a range derived from it. Views cannot be stored inside aggregates. Creation, further borrowing, length and scalar-byte reads allocate nothing and perform no RC; `.copy()` materializes independent owned storage. Unoptimized and optimized builds enforce the same source contract.

- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-CONTAINERS-1] Owned dictionaries and sets own their keys and values. Insertion moves inputs, reads borrow, replacement drops the old value, and pop transfers the removed entry. Explicit copies recursively duplicate owned children, including active union/option payloads and recursive boxes. Pattern bindings borrow their matched owner and cannot escape it. Recursive copies and destruction use generated type-specific callbacks and iterative runtime worklists, without reference counting. Source tuple destructuring is retired by TUPLE-REMOVED-1; certain record migrations stage owned fields before destination writes and preserve result-handling obligations.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-DEFAULT-BORROWS-1] Under checked ownership, `dict.get(key, default)` evaluates its key and default exactly once, eagerly, and borrows the selected managed value for local reads and ordinary borrowed calls. Two-argument text `min`/`max` and aggregate `Option.unwrap_or` likewise evaluate both inputs exactly once in source order and borrow the selected value; optional defaults remain eager. Both possible input owners remain valid through the last use, including nested selections. Selection adds no implicit copy, reference count or runtime marker allocation. Moving, replacing or mutating either owner while the selected borrow remains live is rejected, as are borrowed-result returns, storage in owning aggregates and escaping derived views. An explicit `.copy()` creates independent storage. The exact versioned compiler marker, complete signature and producer shape are validated on every compilation path; malformed markers and consuming uses refuse compilation, and invalid cached forms are discarded.

- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-RESOURCES-1] Zero-RC scoped files borrow their backing provider through normal and early-return cleanup. Scope cleanup completes and destroys the handle while the provider remains live, and result adapters explicitly transfer owned payloads out of their temporary boxes. Root providers own their state without reference counting.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-PAYLOAD-TRANSFER-1] Under checked ownership, `try` consumes its Result/Option and transfers the active payload without an implicit deep copy. Recursive payloads are loaded into an independent value home before freeing only their box shell; their owned descendants move with the value. The rule covers success, propagated errors, options, loop-carried values and mutable-call writebacks. Borrowed pattern bindings remain borrowed and explicit `.copy()` still creates independent owned storage.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-MATCH-TRANSFER-1] Under checked ownership, a `match` directly on a call, callback invocation, variant construction, collection `.copy()`/`.pop()`, or a built-in text/byte decoder, scalar counter or numeric parser consumes that fresh union and transfers its active payload binding. Compiler-generated expression blocks preserve this classification. Bindings can move into returns, locals and containers exactly once; ignored payloads are destroyed. Matching a named value or a field/index projection continues to borrow, even at its last use. A fielded pattern splits its whole fresh payload under NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-RECORD-UNPACK-1. Checking and lowering share this syntactic rule; no last-use inference or ownership annotation is required.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-RECORD-UNPACK-1] Under checked ownership, a named-field pattern on a fresh owned enum match splits its entire hidden payload record before entering the arm. Each binding receives independent ownership of its field exactly once; discarded or unused fields are destroyed. The hidden whole record cannot be used again. Recursive boxed fields transfer their contents into an independent value home before freeing the box shell. Returning, storing or propagating bound values needs no deep copy. Named or projected matches still borrow, ordinary field moves remain limited to the whole-carrier affine case in OWNERSHIP-CONTRACT-1. Nested patterns continue to borrow their internally named intermediate value. This rule requires neither source lifetime annotations nor partial-move tracking.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-RESOURCE-SCOPES-1] Under checked ownership, a dependent Filesystem, Network or Environment capability is bound by `with parent.dir(...)`, `with parent.host(...)`, `with parent.read_only()` or `with parent.only(...)`; the parent must be a named local or an ordinary record field rooted in a named local. The scope borrows the whole enclosing owner and prevents moves/replacement through every exit. Scoped capabilities and files cannot be moved or returned. Cleanup consumes the child while borrowing its parent, innermost first, including return, `try`, break and continue. Root capabilities own their provider; dependent capabilities own only their copied scope bytes. Scoped read-only files release their handle without requesting completion rights. Direct C extern calls remain scalar-only; native provider callbacks borrow call buffers, successful acquisition transfers one handle, successful completion consumes it, and release consumes an uncompleted handle. Propagation transfers an owned active payload from its Result/Option wrapper (NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-PAYLOAD-TRANSFER-1).
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-TASKS-1] At the Core task boundary, a scoped group receives each capture box by move. A task borrows its capture during execution and publishes an independent owned result; completion destroys the capture. Taking a result transfers it out of the group. Ending or dropping a group joins all pending tasks before destroying untaken results and handles. The group cannot escape into a capture. Scheduler synchronization atomics remain; memory ownership uses no reference counts. Source task syntax and borrowed task captures are unsupported.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-TASKS-2] The reserved `parallel.map(values, function)` standard function borrows distinct input elements for scoped tasks. Callbacks accept a read-only input and return an independent storable value. Map preserves input order and joins every task before returning the owned output list; no handles or borrows escape. Contiguous chunks bound scheduler captures, handles and result containers to at most four per configured worker (one for a single worker), while output and temporary chunk element storage scale with input length. Trap diagnostics retain the original input index. Unit elements and general closure captures are unsupported.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-ABI-1] The sole runtime ABI uses checked ownership and has no reference-count field: kind occupies byte offset zero, and alignment keeps the complete header at 32 bytes. Native linking always selects this runtime, without a memory-mode define or alternate runtime source. Legacy retain, release, publication and count-dependent copy-on-write implementations are removed; emitted LLVM contains no reference-count declarations or operations. Static storage uses a kind bit. Unique owners are destroyed directly; allocator and scheduler synchronization remains permitted.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-CACHE-1] Lowering, optimizer, emission and test cache identities retain the constant ownership mode `zero-rc`. Generated copy identities bind type structure; native identities bind the sole runtime ABI and implementation. Successful ownership checks cache per function under compiler, entry, mode, dependency and body identities. Complete typed bodies use a conservative declaration-catalog/body digest fallback. Body omission requires semantic dependency revalidation, ownership proof and, for builds, a lowering artifact proof. Missing or damaged proofs force checking; refusals are rendered fresh. Warm builds match uncached output and remain free of reference-count operations.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-LIBRARY-1] Admitted text/byte helper calls borrow inputs and return independent owned results or immutable static characters. Slicing, trimming, case conversion, splitting, joining, replacement, formatting, hexadecimal conversion and UTF-8 decoding use no retained aliases. Environment stand-ins copy their backing name/value bytes. JSON `get` and `at` borrow their input container and copy only the selected result; missing keys, wrong variants and out-of-range indices return `None` without copying a container. JSON `as_list` and `as_dict` continue to return independent container copies. Unadmitted standard-library operations are compile-time refusals, never RC fallbacks.

- [OWNED-CAPACITY-BOUNDS-1] Runtime block allocation and list growth reject negative capacity or element sizes, unrepresentable element-byte products, header or alignment overflow, and growth past representable capacity through the existing out-of-memory or test memory-budget path before allocating or copying; ordinary growth preserves contents and direct cleanup.

- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-TEST-1] Plain `test FILE|DIR` validates checked ownership before test-key reuse, lowers guest adapters and roots using the sole runtime ABI, and runs directory children with the same default. Local, portable and indexed receipts retain the constant ownership mode in their identities; invalid source cannot reuse a success from an incompatible compiler or contract.

- [OWNED-LIST-MUTATION-1] Checked zero-RC list insert, reverse, indexed pop and remove move existing element storage without duplicating child owners. Order and original index diagnostics are preserved. Popped values transfer ownership; discarded or removed managed children are destroyed exactly once.
- [OWNED-LIST-EXCHANGE-1] `values.exchange(index, replacement)` replaces one existing list element and returns its former value. The index and replacement are evaluated once, in that order, before the current receiver is updated. Explicit negative positions follow NEGATIVE-POSITION-METHOD-1; an out-of-range index traps. Under checked ownership, the replacement moves into the slot and the old element transfers to the result without copying other elements. A fresh match can consume that result. Borrowed or overlapping managed replacements are refused. Discarded results are destroyed exactly once.

- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-AFFINE-RESOURCES-1] Affine resource parameters consume one owner; whole affine Results and records move through calls, returns and owned match or unpack. A function returning a dependent resource carrier has exactly one capability or resource input and preserves that provider origin. Local-provider escape, ambiguous origins and provider replacement or movement while dependent owners remain live refuse. Named provider values remain alive through lexical cleanup on normal, early, propagated-error and loop exits; moves clear the former binding before subsequent lifetime uses. Native acquisition and I/O return independent result storage; completion success destroys the owner and failure transfers it exactly once.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-PROVIDER-FIELDS-1] Owning provider values may occupy ordinary record fields. Field operations borrow the entire enclosing root through dependent scopes and local resources; root moves or replacement are forbidden while those borrows remain live. The enclosing value cannot be explicitly copied. Aggregate parameters borrow, and returned dependent resources still require the existing single direct provider/resource input contract. Lists, dictionaries, fixed arrays, variants, boxed provider storage and arbitrary partial moves remain unsupported. Compiler-only lifetime markers preserve direct cleanup order without heap wrappers or runtime reference counts.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-OWNED-MUT-CALL-1] Checked interior `mut` calls transfer the selected managed child and reattach the returned child through the same captured parent and index path. Selection is evaluated once, and existing source overlap and invalidation rules remain enforced.

- [OWNED-LIST-COPYING-1] Under checked ownership, list slices and extension of copyable elements produce independent destination children. Self-extension reads the finite original prefix through any destination growth. Pending Results and resource owners are never implicitly duplicated by slicing or extension.

- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-RESULT-LISTS-1] Storage eligibility is independent of pending Result handling. Lists may store Results whose payloads contain no owned resource or capability. Moves, returns and calls transfer the whole-list obligation; explicit `_ = results` acknowledges and destroys any remaining elements. Length, indexing and direct iteration borrow a named list without acknowledging that obligation. Abandonment, overwriting and early exits with a pending list are rejected. Explicit `.copy()` creates independent storage and a second obligation; `.pop()` transfers one element while the source list remains pending. Clear, slicing, extension, reversed, zip, implicit list construction and sorting do not duplicate or silently discard pending Results and are refused for such lists. Scoped `parallel.map` accepts storable inputs and results, preserves Result-list obligations, joins before return and destroys acknowledged untaken results exactly once.

- [DECL-DEFINITE-ASSIGNMENT-1] An annotated local binding may omit its initializer; every continuing path must assign before a read. A `const` binding is assigned at most once on each reachable path; unannotated bindings require an initializer.
- [MIGRATION-SUPPORT-1] Migration helpers choose collision-free names, preserve nested indentation, comments and source origins, and withhold hoisting across effects, traps or unstable reads; checked purity excludes allocating constant getters, and original-module coordinates keep generated names stable across project entry roots.
- [SOURCE-TYPES-1] Migration type spellings remove only the owning original module prefix at complete qualified-token boundaries. Nested local type arguments become local names; external qualified names and longer identifier prefixes remain unchanged.

- [REPETITION-1] Sequence multiplication and augmented multiplication produce E_REPETITION_REMOVED with certain fixes to strings.repeat, byte_builder.repeat or lists.filled. List augmented repetition uses explicit snapshot, count and loops, evaluating the original list then count once and copying elements independently; nonpositive counts yield empty sequences.
- [LIST-MEMBERSHIP-1] List membership, including `x in d.values()` because `values()` returns a list, produces E_LIST_MEMBERSHIP_REMOVED; certain pure operands migrate to lists.contains, retaining negation and keyed, text and byte membership.
- [STDLIB-LISTS-1] lists.filled produces independent values and is empty for a nonpositive count, lists.contains compares elements, including Unit under the equality requirement, and lists.last returns the final element or None.

- [DOMAIN-REMOVED-1] Scalar domain declarations, source wrap/unwrap, type-owned operations, codecs and named constraints report E_DOMAIN_REMOVED after typing. Certain fixes introduce ordinary one-field records, free functions and explicit callbacks, preserving module qualification and renaming collisions. The primary diagnostic still states that scalar domains were removed when unrelated diagnostics defer its automatic migration.

- [FIX-DIRECTORY-ROUNDS-1] Directory migration collects fixes from every independent entry before applying each round, so changes to a shared declaration preserve repairs for every caller; preview leaves every file unchanged, and the migrated directory checks cleanly with an idempotent second preview.

- [NEGATIVE-INDEX-FIX-1] Constant negative list, text, bytes and fixed-array indexes and bounds, including uninstantiated generic bodies, report `E_NEGATIVE_INDEX_REMOVED`; certain pure-local-base edits spell `len(base) - k` for indexes and `max(len(base) - k, 0)` for bounds. `selka fix` previews without writing, migrates to clean checking and is idempotent.
- [NEGATIVE-INDEX-FIX-2] Negative-index migration withholds edits for impure or global-getter bases and where an earlier bound could change the base before a later bound is evaluated. A typed base must be provably free of calls, allocations and traps under the shared migration certainty rules.
- [NEGATIVE-INDEX-TRAP-1] A runtime negative list, text, bytes or fixed-array index, including reads and writes, traps with `R_LIST_BOUNDS` or `R_TEXT_BOUNDS`, preserves the written index and length.
- [NEGATIVE-SLICE-TRAP-1] An explicit runtime negative slice endpoint traps before slicing, even for an empty source or reversed interval; a negative step remains valid and omitted endpoints remain defaults.
- [NEGATIVE-POSITION-METHOD-1] Explicit negative list `insert`, `exchange` and indexed `pop` positions trap; `pop()` still removes the tail.
- [NONNEGATIVE-BOUND-1] Nonnegative slice endpoints beyond the length clamp to the length; omitted reverse-slice endpoints preserve reversal, and unsigned bounds are clamped before conversion to the signed slice representation.
- [NEGATIVE-DICT-KEY-1] Negative integer dictionary keys remain valid keys, including inside generic bodies.
- [NEGATIVE-INDEX-LLVM-1] List index LLVM uses the written index with one unsigned bounds comparison; the old negative-index wrap branch, sum, select and join are absent.

- [NEGATIVE-TRAP-OPERANDS-1] Located bounds traps may carry an integer index and integer length as Core operands; use tracking retains both, while the verifier rejects noninteger, partial or nonvoid payloads and payloads without the bounds-trap marker and preserves existing no-operand traps.

- [NEGATIVE-GUARD-NOALLOC-1] Explicit terminating negative-bound guards preserve receiver/index order and report the original signed index and receiver length without allocating; guards retain noalloc borrowing contracts, including text views, in debug and verified release builds.

- [NEGATIVE-INDEX-INLINE-1] A checked read-only scalar list projection in one block retains bounded CFG inlining after removal of negative-index normalization. The existing one-read, one-return, operation whitelist and size limits still apply; mutating and owned one-block functions remain excluded.
- [REPETITION-ORIGIN-1] A repetition helper or legacy Result-match temporary in an imported module gets the same collision-free name and edits from every independent entry root. Directory fixing coalesces those repairs, preserves preview bytes, checks every entry and remains idempotent.

- [FIX-DIRECTORY-DOMAIN-PHASE-1] If any independent entry reports a non-domain error or defers scalar-domain migration because required type facts are unavailable, a fix round withholds every domain repair across the invocation while applying independent repairs. This includes parser and early frontend errors that prevent the domain pass from running. Every entry is rechecked before domain declarations, constraints, signatures or callers change, preserving shared metadata for inferred callback migration.
- [FIX-DOMAIN-MEMBER-CALLBACK-1] When lifting a generic domain member with op requirements, repair both inferred and explicit type-argument callers, preserving module qualification and passing the selected callbacks.

- [NEGATIVE-RANGE-BOUNDS-1] Bounded text/byte search and byte numeric/scalar-count ranges reject negative positional bounds in direct, named and generic calls, preserving original bounds trap values and pure-base certain edits. Nonnegative upper bounds, omitted endpoints, empty-needle past-end rules, evaluation order and parse grammar/overflow/UTF8 results remain unchanged. Explicit negative append_range endpoints and find_any starts trap; nonnegative invalid append_range ranges still return false without writing.
- [FIX-DOMAIN-OWNER-KIND-1] Domain retirement resolves owned functions only for declared source domains; ordinary record/variant constructors, internal float/callback types and resources retain their existing interpretation.
- [FIX-DOMAIN-FORWARDING-1] Callback migration of an inferred generic call forwards the enclosing type parameter's operation independently of concrete specialization, including matching nested collection types and normalized named/default operands; when source-level provider identity cannot be established, preserve provider and constraint metadata and emit guidance without edits.

- [DOC-HISTORY-1] Historical documents remain frozen when language syntax retires. The doctest collector excludes `docs/legacy/`, `docs/audits/`, `docs/design/`, `docs/adr/` and `docs/experiments/`, plus Markdown basenames containing a hyphen- or period-delimited `proposal`, `handoff`, `implementation`, `audit`, `progress` or `plan` word; a filter cannot re-enable them. Documentation coverage rejects implemented-feature pointers into the same excluded pages. Active guides formerly kept there move to a live documentation path while the historical original remains unchanged.
- [NEGATIVE-SLICE-PROOF-1] Slice-copy elimination may prove an immutable endpoint parameter nonnegative after a dominating negative guard whose taken arm performs an exact terminating bounds trap for that parameter and source. A check on another path, after the observation point, with a different source or index, or without an exact check cannot establish this proof; mutable and loop-carried values require separate facts. The original bounds traps remain executable.
- [NEGATIVE-SPLIT-PREFIX-1] Fixed nonnegative split-view indexes retain stack-prefix planning after negative-index normalization is removed. Detached integer constants need no defining CFG block; helper ordering and cache identity remain sensitive to the selected prefix plan.

- [LAMBDA-MIGRATION-1] A noncapturing lambda reports E_LAMBDA_REMOVED with a certain named-function repair using explicit parameter and result types. Generated names are stable in the original module across independent entry roots; helpers precede the enclosing declaration and its leading test comments. The repair preserves evaluation and diagnostics and withholds a fix when captures or incomplete typing prevent proof.

- [CONTROL-EXPRESSIONS-1] Conditional and match expressions are retired with removal diagnostics and certain statement-level repairs that preserve branch laziness and evaluate the match target once. Repairs use definitely assigned declarations or return from each arm; hoisting is offered only when surrounding evaluation order and execution frequency are preserved.

- [COMPREHENSION-1] Retired list, set and dictionary comprehensions and sum/min/max generators carry typed explicit-loop repairs preserving source, filter and value evaluation order, including empty min/max traps.
- [COMPREHENSION-2] Generator any/all repairs exit every nested loop at the first deciding value and preserve short-circuit evaluation.
- [COMPREHENSION-3] Generic and imported comprehension repairs derive accumulator types from source-level abstract parameters independently of the first concrete specialization.

- [CONTROL-COMPOSITION-1] Control-flow expression migration composes with retired lambdas and comprehensions: nested selections retain type facts until their enclosing removal can create a safe statement context, then migrate without changing evaluation order. Multiline lambda replacements preserve following statement separators; generated type names use the original module scope, and unsupported outer contexts retain no-write diagnostics.

- [SYNTAX-RETIREMENT-CACHE-1] Retiring source syntax changes the frontend syntax cache and semantic summary/module interface formats. Prior-format artifacts miss rather than replaying pre-retirement syntax or semantic facts; new-format round trips preserve the current contract.

- [TUPLE-REMOVED-1] Source tuples are retired with E_TUPLE_REMOVED and certain record-return, direct-destructuring and swap repairs. Shared signatures and generated record/field names remain stable across entry roots; missing type facts, unsupported consumers or unrelated errors defer tuple edits atomically, after domain migration. Loop forms enumerate, zip and dictionary items remain supported without materialized tuples.
- [RECORD-KEY-1] Record sort keys compare recursively orderable scalar fields in declaration order, preserving stable ties and reverse ordering for nested, empty and wide records; hashable record fields support dictionary and set keys while retaining nominal type identity.

- [EFFECTS-REMOVED-1] The retired effects(...), noio and trailing noalloc clauses report E_EFFECTS_REMOVED with certain migration fixes. Callback, operation and extern ceilings are erased; body-bearing functions preserve allocation promises as @noalloc.

- [NOALLOC-ATTRIBUTE-1] `@noalloc` attaches to a function with a body and feeds the Core allocation assertion in FRONTEND-CORE-NOALLOC-1. Allocating, unsupported and unresolved paths report E_NOALLOC_ALLOCATES and fail build, run and test before execution.

- [CONTRACT-CAPABILITY-1] A contract condition cannot mention capability parameters or resolved capability fields, including direct receivers, projections and helper arguments.

- [TEST-RECEIPT-CAPABILITY-1] Persistent private and shared test receipt lookup and publication require complete proof of no live Host input across reachable calls. Unknown callback targets and program-wide extern declarations refuse reuse. Compiler identity invalidates IR receipts; runner and linked-program identity invalidate execution receipts, with all eligibility dependencies bound into those identities.

- [TUPLE-CLAUSE-RETIREMENT-CACHE-1] Retiring tuple and effect-clause syntax changes frontend syntax and semantic summary/module interface formats. Prior-format artifacts miss instead of replaying retired syntax or effect facts. Compiler identity invalidates IR/settings receipts; runner identity binds the complete no-live-input eligibility proof for execution receipts.

- [TYPED-METADATA-CACHE-1] Removing unreachable codec and positional-normalization metadata changes frontend syntax, semantic summary/module interface and checked-body formats. Prior-format artifacts miss instead of replaying removed typed fields; current-format round trips and compiler/runner identity invalidation retain their existing contracts.

- [POSITION-BOUNDS-1] Every explicit negative sequence index, slice or search bound, bytes.find_any start, and byte-buffer append_range endpoint traps with R_LIST_BOUNDS or R_TEXT_BOUNDS and the written index and sequence length. The terminating failure survives optimization. Omitted stepped-slice endpoints retain their defaults; nonnegative bounds keep their existing upper-clamp or invalid-range behavior.
- [DECL-CONST-DEFINITE-1] A local `const name: T` may omit its initializer. Every path reaching a read must first assign the binding; each reachable path may assign it only once. A write after a possibly assigned branch or on a repeating loop path is rejected with a diagnostic identifying the declaration.
- [CAPABILITY-UNUSED-1] An unused capability parameter whose name starts with `_` is accepted, including callback implementations retaining their required signature. Other unused capability parameters report `E_UNUSED_CAPABILITY` with a structured certain rename fix that prefixes `_`.
- [DECL-MANAGED-EARLY-RETURN-1] A managed local declared without an initializer is cleaned up only on paths where it was assigned; early returns before assignment neither leak nor destroy an uninitialized value under checked ownership.
- [DOMAIN-REMOVAL-MESSAGE-1] Scalar-domain removal diagnostics always state that scalar domains and type-owned operations were removed, including when unrelated errors defer automatic migration.
- [RESERVED-NAME-1] A reserved word where a name is expected (a function, parameter or local name such as `wrap`, `unwrap` or `todo`) is reported as `E_SYNTAX` whose message names the word and says it is reserved, instead of the bare "expected a name"; a bare `wrap` that is not followed by `[` is reported the same way at its call site.
- [MKDIR-ERROR-REASON-1] A refused directory operation names why, on the host filesystem and on the in-memory stand-in alike: `mkdir` of a path that exists says `already exists`, of one whose parent is missing says `the parent directory does not exist`, of one under a file says `a parent of the path is not a directory`; `listdir` of a file says `is not a directory`; `remove` of a directory with entries says `the directory is not empty`. Refusals by grants and the missing-path wording of FILE-ERROR-REASON-1 are unchanged.
- [TEST-DIR-UNCHECKED-1] A test file in a directory run that does not check or build is reported once: `failed FILE: failed to check or build, so no tests ran (the diagnostic is printed above)`, counted as one failure, with no second "test-declaring source was not loaded by any test program" line for the same file. A program that exits 2 before reporting, or that exits otherwise without a usable report, keeps its own wording. The other files of the directory still run and report as before.
- [LINT-CONSTANTS-1] `selka lint` judges a module constant as a value, not a function. A private constant is `dead-private` only when no name in its module reads it (a scalar constant's reads fold away, so the call graph shows none); an exported constant is `dead-export` only when no name or `module.NAME` field reads it; `naming` accepts `UPPER_SNAKE_CASE` and `snake_case` for a constant and says `constant NAME` when it fails; and `repeated-literal` counts only literals the source writes, not reads of a named constant.
- [COMPREHENSION-4] A retired comprehension that is an argument of a call, or of a method call on a string or byte literal (`", ".join([...])`), carries its loop repair when every operand evaluated before it is a stable literal (integer, boolean, unit, string or byte), so hoisting the loop ahead of the statement preserves evaluation order; a receiver that is a variable, or an earlier operand that reads one, leaves the diagnostic without a fix.
- [REFERENCE-CODES-1] `selka reference --query CODE` answers a diagnostic code: the text answer prints `diagnostic CODE` with its one-line meaning and the `selka docs` guide that covers it, and the JSON envelope carries a `diagnostics` array of `{code, meaning, guide}`. Matching is a case-insensitive substring of the code, and an exact code is an `exact` match. Every `E_`, `W_`, `L_` and `R_` code printed by the compiler or its runtime has a row in `src/diagnostic_codes.zig`, sorted by code, naming a real `selka docs` topic; a unit test scans the sources and fails when a printed code has no row. The `selka --help` example is a real code.
- [CHECK-SUCCESS-LINE-1] A successful `selka check` writes one line to standard error, `check: ok (N modules, M cached)`, after any warnings: N is the modules the check loaded, M is all of them when an identical project check was replayed from its stored verdict and otherwise the modules whose syntax was served from the cache. Standard output stays empty and the exit status is unchanged; `--diagnostics=json` writes no such line, a failing check writes none, and `selka fix` does not repeat the line of the final check it runs.
- [FILE-ERROR-REASON-2] The in-memory `filesystem.memory()` stand-in refuses with the same reasons as the host filesystem, in the words of the one table of the system's errors (FILE-ERROR-REASON-1): a path with nothing at it says `the path does not exist`, writing below a folder that is not there says `a folder on the way does not exist`, a full memory says `no space left on the device`, reading a directory says `is a directory`, a path under a file says `a parent of the path is not a directory`, and `listdir` of a file says `is not a directory`; a path outside the root (a `..` past it, or an absolute path) says `denied: 'PATH' is outside the granted directories`, while `.`, `..` inside it, `//` and a trailing slash mean what they mean to the system.
